# MS.990 Parallel Edition: Canvases 65–94

## Page 94 — IIIF Canvas #65

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0065.JP2` (5437 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0065.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0065.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 94 (recto, numbered '94' in upper-right margin). Column A finishes the wonderful eye-water (boil 12 hours, cured a man blind for 10 years in 40 days!), gives 'khöstliches augen Waser für fell vnd Maill', 'Ain anders gar khöstliches augen Waser' (sal ammoniac, alum, white vitriol, celandine, eyebright, cornflower, aged in a brass pot until sky-blue, then sun-distilled), and 'Ain Anders für die fell'. Column B lists powder/drop variants, 'Saffier Waser für Maill vnd fell' (limewater + sal ammoniac in a brass vessel turning sapphire-blue), and 'Das grüene augen Waser' (virgin honey, vitriol, sal ammoniac, white lead, aged in a brass vessel until green, then sun-distilled). Transcribed with Gemini 4.

## Page 95 — IIIF Canvas #66

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0066.JP2` (5554 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0066.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0066.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 95 (verso, numbered '95' in upper-left margin). Column A gives 'Ain Anders für maill vnd fell' (white vitriol and alum boiled in a freshly laid egg), 'Volgen noch andre mitel Vor die fell' (celandine, eyebright, vervain, fennel, gall, alum, vitriol, sal ammoniac, sugar candy, sugar of lead), and opens 'Von den Waser fellen' (hydropic/watery corneal/conjunctival swelling resembling a soft leather bag). Column B displays Portrait '.C.' ('Figur der Waser fell') showing a young woman with a red-beaded necklace, pearl drop earrings, and a golden forehead jewel/spot, followed by 'die khur diser fell ist dises' (purgation, bloodletting, vesicatoria) and 'Ain guette Lattwerung in den Waser fellen'. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait C: Young Woman Illustrating 'Waser Fell' (p. 95, Col. B Upper)** (`#xywh=2700,420,2400,3960`): Half-column watercolour bust portrait labeled '.C.' and titled 'Figur der Waser fell', showing a young woman wearing a red headdress with green foliage, a pearl drop earring, a red-beaded necklace, and a pale watery swelling on her eyes.

## Page 96 — IIIF Canvas #67

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0067.JP2` (5469 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0067.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0067.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 96 (recto, numbered '96' in upper-right margin). Column A gives four remedies for watery films: 'Ain gar guettes Wasser für dise fell' (hematite, red coral, ivory, rose-water), 'Ain anders Wasser' (coral, tutty, sugar candy in Malmsey), 'Ain ander khöstliches Wasser' (mallow, elderflower, tormentil, alum, vitriol), 'Ain anders guettes Wasser' (larkspur, chicory, cornflower, camphor), and 'Ain guets Pulluer für die Waser fell'. Column B gives an NB warning against irritating inflamed eyes with sharp waters (offering a cooling strawberry/endive/camphor/alum collyrium), then opens 'Von den Blüett vnd fleisch fellen der augen' (pterygium / Sebel / panniculus carnosus), requiring rapid surgical treatment and bloodletting. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

## Page 97 — IIIF Canvas #68

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0068.JP2` (5533 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0068.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0068.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 97 (verso, numbered '97' in upper-left margin). Column A prescribes 'Ain guets Waser für die fleisch fell' (celandine, burnt alum, verdigris), 'Ain anders Edles Pulluer' (hematite, burnt ivory, myrrh, long pepper, white vitriol), 'Ain anders Pulluer', and then opens the surgical excision of advanced pterygium (seating the patient in a low armchair bound by hands and feet). Column B displays a watercolour figure of a mustachioed man in a white doublet and green breeches seated in a red-backed wooden armchair ready for pterygium surgery, and below details passing a curved needle with a silk thread ('seiden faden') under the fleshy film to lift and excise it cleanly without piercing the cornea or conjunctiva. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

## Page 98 — IIIF Canvas #69

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0069.JP2` (5433 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0069.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0069.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 98 (recto, numbered '98' in upper-right margin). Column A displays Portrait '.D.' ('Figur der fleisch fell') showing a mustachioed man in a gold-trimmed tunic with intensely red vascular pterygia encroaching across both eyes, followed by surgical completion: lifting the silk thread in the left hand and excising the pterygium with a sharp knife without cutting the thread or injuring the globe, plus a styptic collyrium. Column B gives a healing egg-white/terra-sigillata plaster and 'Augen Wasser Zue den abgenomen fleisch fellen', then opens 'Von den Mükhen oder fliegen maill der augen' (dark corneal spots/maculae resembling flies or gnats). Transcribed with Gemini 4.

## Page 99 — IIIF Canvas #70

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0070.JP2` (5519 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0070.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0070.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 99 (verso, numbered '99' in upper-left margin). Column A finishes describing fly-spot corneal maculae (which obscure vision like a cobweb or thread), displays Portrait '.E.' ('Figur der Mukh Maill': young woman with a green leaf hair ornament and red ribbon choker), and begins 'Ain guets Tranckh die scharffe feüghtigkhait Zü digeriern'. Column B finishes the digestive decoction and cassia purge, gives 'Ain khöstliches Waser für dise Maill', 'Ain anders Waser', 'Ain guettes Pulluer' (white frankincense, mastic, burnt crab-eyes, amber, camphor), and opens the major treatise on chronic hot and cold ocular fluxes ('Von den bösen vnd lang wehrenten augen flüsen. Rinen. hizigen vnd Rinnen der augen'). Transcribed with Gemini 4.

## Page 100 — IIIF Canvas #71

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0071.JP2` (5405 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0071.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0071.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 100 (recto, numbered '100' in upper-right margin). Column A displays Portrait '.F.' of a woman with fiercely bloodshot, weeping eyes and tear streaks down her cheeks, explaining below that chronic fluxes are of two kinds—hot and cold—and detailing hot fluxes eroding the eyeball and causing blindness. Column B prescribes 'Ain guettes Tranckh Zü den hizigen augen flüsen' (wild chicory, dodder, fumitory, rue, borage), Electuarium de succo rosarum, cupping/venesection, 'Ain guette Bähung in hizigen augen flüß', 'Ain guettes Waser für die haisen flüß in die augen', and 'Ain anders khöstliches Waser für die hizigen flüß'. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait F: Woman with Hot Weeping Ocular Fluxes (p. 100, Col. A Upper)** (`#xywh=200,220,2360,3180`): Half-column watercolour bust portrait labeled '.F.' depicting a curly-haired woman with a gold neckband, intensely reddened conjunctivae, and tear streaks running down both cheeks.

Page 101 (verso, numbered '101' in upper-left margin). Column A gives 'Ain Guette Bähung', 'Ain guets augen Wasser', cooling compresses for hot eye fluxes, 'Waser für Entzintung vnd schmerzen der augen' (with opium and women's milk), and 'Ain anders' (baked/roasted apple poultice with butter, sugar, and saffron). Column B adds 'Ain anders für hiz vnd schmerzen' (adding 2–3 grains opium if pain is severe), 'Ain anders für grosse Schmerzen vnd flüß Von geblüedt', 'dises stelt den flus auf', 'Ain anders Wo Zü ruth seind', 'Ain anders so den flus Zurükh helt', an NB on purging and applying fennel/vervain/rose infusion, and opens 'für Grosse Schmerzen'. ## Page 102 — IIIF Canvas #73

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0073.JP2` (5486 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0073.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0073.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 102 (recto, numbered '102' in upper-right margin). Column A covers fenugreek mucilage, 'für Rotte augen' (camphor, tutty, aloes, white sugar, rose-water), 'Ain anders der gleichen', 'Ain anders für schmerzen vnd Rötte', 'Ain anders für flüßige rodte vnd rinnente augen' (fresh wormwood on flax pledget with egg-white), 'Sälbel für den hizigen fluß Wunder bahrlich' (fresh butter, camphor, tutty, sugar candy washed in vinegar), and 'Ain anders khöstliches Sälbel'. Column B completes a honey/camphor/butter salve, gives 'Defensiuo für flüßige augen', 'Ain anders für die flüß', 'für Schmerzen der augen', 'für Schmerzen vnd Frenen der augen', 'Ung. Neapoleum', guaiacum wood-drink ('lignum sanctum'), and 'für Endtzindung der augen lider'. Transcribed with Gemini 4.

## Page 103 — IIIF Canvas #74

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0074.JP2` (5483 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0074.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0074.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 103 (verso, numbered '103' in upper-left margin). Column A finishes the white-bread/egg-yolk eyelid cataplasm, gives 'Ain anders' (mullein, bean flower, rue, violet, rose waters), 'Ain anders für Endtzintung schmerzen vnd fliessen', 'Ain anders', and 'Ain Wunderbahrliches Waser für Maill vnd flüß' (lime-water, egg-whites, sal ammoniac beaten in a brass vessel until green, illustrated by the brass vessels on Col. B). Column B displays a watercolour of three gleaming brass/gold basins on a shelf ('fig_p103_three_brass_vessels'), followed by 'Ain anders für augen hiz vnd Rötte' (sugar of lead, alum-egg) and an important clinical 'NB' warning oculists to distinguish cold fluxes (requiring warm steamings/baths) from hot salty fluxes (requiring cooling, bloodletting, and purging). Transcribed with Gemini 4.

**Illustration: Three Brass Vessels for Eye-Water Preparation (p. 103, Col. B Upper)** (`#xywh=2700,260,2380,1180`): Watercolour drawing of one large central brass vessel flanked by two smaller brass bowls/cups resting on a wooden shelf, illustrating the brass-vessel beating of sal ammoniac and egg-white collyria.

## Page 104 — IIIF Canvas #75

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0075.JP2` (5616 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0075.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0075.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 104 (recto, numbered '104' in upper-right margin). Column A prescribes 'In hizenden vnd hizigen flüßen ist diß Volgentes sehr herlich guett' (plantain, houseleek, rose, fennel, egg-white, camphor, white vitriol), 'Ain Anders' (steeping bruised rose-hip bark in egg-white), 'Ain Herliches Sälbel' (tutty, camphor, verdigris, washed butter), 'Volgents Sälbel Jst herlich vnd guett' (washed butter, sugar of lead, tutty, calamine, camphor), and 'Ain anders, Herliches Waser Vor die Enzintung der augen'. Column B completes the eye-water (tutty, camphor, red rose, burnet-saxifrage), adds a cooling egg-white/alum/saffron compress, presents 'Volgentes Jst ain Herliches Sälbl' (fresh butter, capon fat, white mercury precipitate, tutty), and opens 'für hizige geschwolne augen' (roasted/boiled apple pulp poultice). Transcribed with Gemini 4.

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0076.JP2` (5504 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0076.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0076.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 105 (verso, numbered '105' in upper-left margin). Column A concludes the poultice from p. 104 and opens 'Von khalten augen flüsen welche dise khumen. vnd wie sÿe Zü Churiern. .G.' above Portrait .G. (woman in blue headscarf weeping watery tears), explaining the three causes of cold ocular fluxes from brain moisture and head weakness, followed by 'Ein Herliches Trankh für khalte augen flüß'. Column B gives 'Ein Sehr Khöstliche Purgation in khalten augen flüsen', bloodletting and vesicatory advice, 'Ein guette haubt Salbung. für die khalte augen flüß', and 'Ein Edles Trankh für khalte flüß'.

Page 106 (recto, numbered '106' in upper-right margin). Column A gives five recipes for cold ocular fluxes: 'Ein Khöstliches wasser für die khalte flüß', 'Ein guettes Trankh. für den khalten flüß', 'Ein guettes augen wasser für die khalte flüß', 'Ein herliches Pülluer für die khalten flüß' (chewed in the mouth), and 'Ein guettes wasser darmit die augen auß Zü waschen'. Column B opens 'Von den alten lang wehr=enden bössen augen flüsen .H.' above Portrait .H. (dark-haired bearded man weeping dark tears), discussing chronic fluxes stemming from childhood smallpox, scurvy, syphilis, and long-standing head pains.

## Page 107 — IIIF Canvas #78

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0078.JP2` (5529 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0078.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0078.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 107 (verso, numbered '107' in upper-left margin). Column A introduces 'Wie man aber Solliche flüß begegen Soll. ist Volgents Zü Vernemben', recommending the neck seton ('Schnüer oder Setaceum oder Laqueum') above Figure .I. (large watercolour plate of the steel screw-clamp, needle, and red silk cord). Column B describes the full surgical technique: heating the needle tip red-hot, pinching up the skin of the nape with the clamp, piercing through the two holes, threading and knotting the buttered crimson silk cord, drawing it daily, and applying an escharotic salve if proud flesh threatens to close the wound.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Instrument Plate .I.: Seton Clamp, Needle, and Red Silk Cord (p. 107, Col. A)** (`#xywh=380,2240,2280,4220`): Full-column watercolour instrument plate labeled '.I.' depicting the steel seton screw-clamp with a pointed needle passed through its lower jaws and a long twisted red crimson silk cord.

Page 108 (recto, numbered '108' in upper-right margin). Column A displays Portrait .K. ('Figur wie die Schnüer in den hall Soll gelegt werden'), showing a weeping man in profile with the seton clamp attached to his nape and the red silk cord in place, followed by post-operative care with rose oil, diapalma plaster, digestive salve, and 'Ein herliches Sälbel Zur Schnüer Wan es mit fleisch will Züe=wachsen'. Column B adds Unguentum Apostolorum, Unguentum Aegyptiacum, 'Volgentes Sälbel Ist scharff vnd guett den flüß her wider Zü bring[en]', prognosis for desperate chronic fluxes, and opens the guaiacum wood-draught ('lignum sanctum') recipe.

## Page 109 — IIIF Canvas #80

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0080.JP2` (5554 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0080.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0080.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 109 (verso, numbered '109' in upper-left margin). Column A concludes the guaiacum wood-draught regimen (luting the pot, boiling, sweating for 2–3 hours for 6–8 weeks, biweekly purgation, monthly vesicatories, diet, and warning not to torment patients whose sight is already lost). Column B opens 'Von der lötzung der augen oder wan es ain aug gestochen wirdt' above Portrait .L. (woman in profile with blue hair ribbon and red coral necklace whose left eye is pierced by a knife), warning of deep wounds to the cornea, uvea, and loss of the crystalline humor.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait .L.: Woman with Penetrating Eye Injury (p. 109, Col. B)** (`#xywh=2680,3560,2440,2900`): Watercolour profile portrait labeled '.L.' depicting a woman with a blue hair ribbon, red coral earring, and red coral necklace, with a small knife piercing her left eye and blood flowing down her cheek.

Page 110 (recto, numbered '110' in upper-right margin). Continues treatments for penetrating ocular wounds and loss of aqueous humor: greater celandine and calcined crayfish powder, camphorated celandine leaf extract with egg white, myrrh/bole in breastmilk, a cataplasm of eyebright/fennel/plantain, the prized 'elm-bladder sap' secret ('NB: dises mörke vnd gib dies groses archanum'), and an emergency salt-water dressing instructing the surgeon to bandage both eyes gently so the humor is not squeezed out.

## Page 111 — IIIF Canvas #82

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0082.JP2` (5536 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0082.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0082.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 111 (verso, numbered '111' in upper-left margin). Column A concludes the wound treatments from p. 110 (lukewarm red-wine bran poultice, beaten egg white, roasted egg-yolk/eyebright collyrium, green eye-shade over the healthy eye, warning against hot medicines in corneal vs. conjunctival wounds, and 'Ein Sehr guettes Pflaster Zü den Verlötzten augen'). Column B completes the tormentil healing water and opens 'Von abnemung des gesichts Blödigkheit dunkhle vnd Triebe der augen', discussing old age ('in old churches one finds dark windows'), phlegm-producing foods (garlic, peas, lentils, old cheese), and thick phlegm obstructing the optic nerve.

## Page 112 — IIIF Canvas #83

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0083.JP2` (5584 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0083.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0083.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 112 (recto, numbered '112' in upper-right margin). Column A discusses clouding of the vitreous and albugineous humors, weeping, looking at fire and snow, reading/writing small script at night, and excessive bloodletting, above Portrait .M. (bust wearing a yellow-orange patterned cap and dark-rimmed spectacles). Below Portrait .M., the text classifies eye colors (black as strongest, blue as weakest yet loveliest, grey as nimble) and eye sizes (small eyes sharper than large protruding eyes). Column B warns against delaying treatment ('Sight is the highest treasure, yet people often treat their eyes as if they had more eyes than fingers on their hand') and gives 'Ein khöstliches Pulver Zü stärkhen aines Schwachen gesicht' and 'Ein Arcana das gesicht Zü Stärkh[en]'.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait .M.: Figure Wearing Dark-Rimmed Spectacles (p. 112, Col. A)** (`#xywh=300,1140,2280,2860`): Watercolour bust portrait labeled '.M.' depicting a person wearing a yellow-orange patterned cap and dark-rimmed nose-bridge spectacles.

## Page 113 — IIIF Canvas #84

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0084.JP2` (5531 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0084.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0084.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 113 (verso, numbered '113.' in upper-left margin). Column A gives age-graded ophthalmic arcana (40–50, 50–60, 60–70, 70–80, 80–100 years), including a small inline watercolour of a wooden drinking cup ('Tränkhlen. Von Eschig [holz]', ash-wood beaker) for restoring sight to the blind, plus golden/cochia pills for severe fluxes. Column B provides 'Ein khöstliches augen Wasser. Wan die augen schwach vnd Blödt sein weiche nicht mögen Zü schreiben oder Zü lesen', 'Augen Wasser Inerlich Zü stärkhen das schwache gesicht' (distilled in malmsey wine), and 'Ein khöstliches Pulver Zur stärkhung des gesichts' that restored a prelate's sight after ten years of blindness.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Ash-Wood Drinking Cup (p. 113, Col. A)** (`#xywh=980,1220,400,400`): Small inline watercolour illustration of a yellow wooden cup/beaker turned by a cooper from ash wood ('Von Eschig').

## Page 114 — IIIF Canvas #85

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0085.JP2` (5549 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0085.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0085.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 114 (recto, numbered '114' in upper-right margin). Continues sight-strengthening recipes: 'Ein absonderliche Lattwerung Zü Stärkhung des gesichts' (eyebright/fennel/celandine electuary), 'Schwaches gesicht Zü stärkhen vnd Zü Conseruiern' (white onion juice & virgin honey, noting patients never needed spectacles again), 'Ein anders die augen Zü stärkhen' (honey boiled with an 8-year-old boy's urine), 'Die Conserua Von flor. Euphrasia', 'Zum Schwachen Gesicht', 'Zü stärkhung der Augen' (soft-roasted egg filled with vitriol), 'Das gesicht Zü stärkhen', and 'Ein herliches vnd khöstliches Wasser Zü stärkhen des gesicht' (malmsey wine, celandine, eyebright, quenched tutty, camphor, and aloes).

## Page 115 — IIIF Canvas #86

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0086.JP2` (5521 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0086.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0086.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 115 (verso, numbered '115.' in upper-left margin). Column A gives an eye-powder of rose sugar and cuttlefish bone, 'Beÿ Solchen augen Schwacheiten müsen auch Zü Zeiten guette haubt vnd augen Pillulen gebraucht werden' (Crato's amber pills with alhandal and diagridium), an herbal water blend, 'Augen stärkhentes Trisenet' (valerian/eyebright/cubeb spice powder), and 'Ein guetes Wasser Welliches das Gesicht hell vnd klar macht'. Column B treats obstructed optic nerves ('Wan die augen Neruen Verstopft brauch dises' with tormentil plaster and fennel eaten fasting), 'Von dunkhle augen', two distilled waters ('Ein guetes Wasser die augen Zü stärkh[en]'), and 'Ein Khöstliche Lattwerung' of fresh eyebright and fennel.

Page 116 (recto, numbered '116' in upper-right margin). Column A gives a medicated wine bag ('Man khan auch Von augen drost Cimet vnd fenchel sam. ain säckhl machn'), eating 20 juniper berries every morning or sugar-coated Roman fennel seed, two spice powders ('Ein Khöstliches Pülluer für abnemb=ung des gesichts'), and 'Ein Säckhlein in Wein Zü henkhen für Schwacheit der augen'. Column B opens 'Dises ist ain Neues stükel Wie man das Verlohrne gesicht wider bring[en] khan' above Figure .N., a full-column watercolour showing a man in a yellow coat leaning over a table with a green bifurcated funnel over an earthenware pot directing celandine steam into both eyes.

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0088.JP2` (5547 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0088.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0088.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 117 (verso, numbered '117' in upper-left margin). Column A concludes the celandine steam-funnel cure from p. 116 and displays Figure .O. ('Figur des Instruments'), a standalone watercolour of the green bifurcated funnel, followed by construction notes, 'Volgentes Wasser Soll man nach dem Bähen in die augen Than', and 'Augen stärkhente Latwerung'. Column B gives 'Gueiden Wein für ain schwaches gesicht', two eye-strengthening spirits ('Ein augen stärkhenten geist' and a hartshorn/saffron/castoreum spirit), 'Augen stärkhente Zeltel' (sugar lozenges with ambergris and musk), and 'Haubt vnd augen stärkhentes äpfel' (an aromatic pomander).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Instrument Plate .O.: Bifurcated Eye-Steaming Funnel (p. 117, Col. A)** (`#xywh=380,1620,2280,1500`): Standalone watercolour instrument illustration labeled '.O.' under 'Figur des Instruments', depicting a green glass conical base splitting into two narrow eye-cups.

## Page 118 — IIIF Canvas #89

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0089.JP2` (5633 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0089.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0089.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 118 (recto, numbered '118' in upper-right margin). Column A gives 'Ein khöstliche Lattwerung Zü stärkhen der augen', 'Ein Schwacheit vnd Blödigkheit der augen' (volatile salts of hartshorn, amber, eyebright, mugwort, fennel, and rosemary; plus a spiced honey electuary held on the tongue so its vapors rise upward), and 'Volgentes haubt Pülluer Jst als ain schnüpff Tobakh Zü brauchen'. Column B adds valerian water in wine, 'augen stärkhente Zeltl', 'Schnüpff Puluer in Schwaheit der augn Zü brauchen', and opens the new chapter 'Von sachen aitern der augen' above Portrait .P. (youth with purulent discharge streaming from both eyes).

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Portrait .P.: Youth with Purulent Eye Discharge (p. 118, Col. B)** (`#xywh=2620,3440,2400,2540`): Watercolour bust portrait labeled '.P.' depicting a curly-haired young person in a brown collar with greyish purulent matter streaming from both eyes.

## Page 119 — IIIF Canvas #90

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0090.JP2` (5575 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0090.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0090.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 119 (verso, numbered '119' in upper-left margin). Column A concludes the etiology of white (melancholic) and yellow (bilious) purulent eye fluxes, prescribing purgation with species dia turbith or cochia/aureae pills, shoulder/nape cupping, and venesection, followed by two eye-bag poultices ('Ein guetes Säckhel auf die augen für aitern vnd Schweren') and two distilled waters ('Ein Guetes Waser für aitern vnd Schweren der augen'). Column B gives two ointments ('Ein Guetes Sälbel für das aitern der augen'), 'Ein guetes Puluer vnd Pflaster in den süren flüß', and opens the new chapter 'Stöchen vnd drükhen der augen' (gritty/sandy sensation from hot humors, blisters, or engorged veins in the conjunctiva).

## Page 120 — IIIF Canvas #91

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0091.JP2` (5492 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0091.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0091.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4

Page 120 (recto, numbered '120' in upper-right margin). Column A concludes the chapter on ocular stinging and pressing ('Stöchen vnd drükhen'), distinguishing humoral vs. sanguine causes and providing four eye-water collyria ('Ein guets Wasser für drükhen vnd Stöchen der augen', 'Ein anders Wasser...', 'Waser Von das Stöchen vnd Drükhen in augen', and 'Ein anders Guets Waser'). Column B opens 'Khizlen vnd Jeiken der augen' (ocular itching and tickling), diagnosing brain vs. blood origin by eye redness, and prescribing 'Pillule Wider das Jeiken vnd Khizlen', 'Bahung Wider das Jeiken vnd Hitzig[en]', and three soothing eye waters.

## Page 121 — IIIF Canvas #92

**Folio / Side:** verso · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0092.JP2` (5587 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0092.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0092.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Verso page (two-column layout) on 'Hiz Pladern der augen' (heat-blisters / phlyctenules of the eyes): etiology, symptoms, and progression on the cornea and conjunctiva, followed by general purgative/bloodletting/vesicant advice and specific recipes for eye waters, an electuary, a salve, and a fumigation.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| 1 | 121. | 121. *— Note: Original page number in a ruled box at top left.* |
| 2 | **[Rubric] Von den Hiz Pladern der** | Of the Heat-Blisters of the *— Note: Red-ink rubric.* |
| 3 | **[Rubric] augen.** | Eyes. *— Note: Red-ink rubric, continuation of heading.* |
| 4 | Die hiz bladern kumben her von disen Vrsach | The heat-blisters come from these causes, *— Note: Vrsach for Ursachen.* |
| 5 | alß vorhero gemelt worden von der Hizen | as previously mentioned, from the heat |
| 6 | vd kizlen der augen, nemlich von yberfliß= | and irritation of the eyes, namely from superfluous *— Note: vd = vnd (und); kizlen = itching/irritation.* |
| 7 | iger gesalzner feüchtigkheit. des hirns vd des | saline humor of the brain and of the |
| 8 | bluedts, welliche sich zwischn der Cornea vd | blood, which settles between the cornea and |
| 9 | coniunctiua legt, daselbige vber legt vd | conjunctiva, covers it over, and |
| 10 | Pläderlein Verursacht. doher dan es geschicht | causes little blisters. Whence it happens |
| 11 | das solche leidt beÿ groser der augen hiz | that such people, with great heat of the eyes *— Note: leidt = Leute (people).* |
| 12 | vd stichlen, röthe, wäsern der augen haben, | and stinging, have redness [and] watering of the eyes, |
| 13 | ach oft das liecht nit erdultn khin. etliche | also often cannot endure the light. Some *— Note: ach = auch; khin = können (photophobia).* |
| 14 | Pläderlein seyn anfangs ganz klain vd weis | little blisters are at first very small and white |
| 15 | außen, so gros als ain mogen khorn. dernach | on the outside, as large as a poppy seed. Afterwards *— Note: mogen khorn = Mohnkorn (poppy seed).* |
| 16 | werden seÿ gelb vd weis, vd zue lezt ganz | they become yellow and white, and at last completely *— Note: seÿ = sie.* |
| 17 | braun. dise Pläderlein braiden sich je lenger | brown. These little blisters spread the longer *— Note: braiden = breiten.* |
| 18 | ie weitter auß, vd über ziehn oft den ganzen | the wider out, and often cover over the entire *— Note: Reading of 'über ziehn' somewhat compressed in Kurrent.* |
| 19 | aug apfl. etliche haben ein, vd machen ain | eyeball. Some sink in, and make a *— Note: ein [fallen/sinken] or ain.* |
| 20 | grüeblein in das Cornea. vd wan man es | little pit in the cornea. And if one *— Note: Corneal ulceration.* |
| 21 | vber sicht, so frist es die Cornea gar an das | neglects it, it eats away the cornea so far that *— Note: vber sicht = übersieht (neglects).* |
| 22 | oft humor vd molt daraus für stößt. oder | often humor and matter protrudes from it, or *— Note: molt = matter/pus (or reading of aqueous humor prolapse).* |
| 23 | es werden die augn mit heiser hiz heben vber= | the eyes are overgrown with hot heat-swelling *— Note: Syntax slightly elliptical; 'heben' or 'heften' before 'vberzogen'.* |
| 24 | zogen. davon der mensch erblindt khan. | from which the person can go blind. |
| 25 | Erstlich solle man woll achtung haben auf | First, one should pay close attention to |
| 26 | wellich heütl das Pläderl sezt. den wan | which membrane the little blister sits upon. For when *— Note: heütl = Häutlein (cornea or conjunctiva).* |
| 27 | solliches erkhant, so brauchet hernach volgente | this is recognized, then use the following |
| 28 | mitl. | remedies. |
| 29 | Erstlich vor dem aber ist vonnötn, das man den | First, however, before that, it is necessary that one |
| 30 | Patient Phurgire. wan aber die Pläderln | purge the patient. But if the little blisters |
| 31 | von geblüet khumen, ader laß schröpfel lase. | come from the blood, let blood [by] venesection [or] cupping. *— Note: ader laß schröpfel lase = Aderlass [oder] Schröpfköpfe setzen lassen.* |
| 32 | hernach die Veschicoria ziehe. hinten den ohrn | Afterwards draw vesicatories behind the ears, *— Note: Veschicoria = vesicatoria (blistering plasters).* |
| 33 | als brauch auch wie volgt. | then also use as follows. |
| 34 | **[Rubric] Ein guets Waser vor die Hiz Plad=** | A Good Water for the Heat-Blisters *— Note: Red-ink rubric.* |
| 35 | **[Rubric] lein der augen.** | of the Eyes. *— Note: Red-ink rubric.* |
| 36 | ℞. melilota alb. ruta. aā ℥j. linse. fen= | [Recipe:] white melilot, rue, of each 1 ounce; flaxseed, fenu- *— Note: ℞ = Recipe; aā = ana; ℥j = 1 ounce; linse = Leinsamen (linseed).* |
| 37 | um Graecum. ros. alb. aā. ℥j. margrandt | greek, white roses, of each 1 ounce; pomegranate |
| 38 | blüemen ℥iβ. XXX. ℥β. Zer stoß ales grob | blossoms 1½ ounces; [camphor/saffron?] ½ ounce. Pound all coarsely, *— Note: XXX = alchemical/apothecary glyph (likely camphor or saffron in this MS); ℥iβ = 1.5 oz; ℥β = 0.5 oz.* |
| 39 | darauf gieß. süß waser ℔j. vd ▽ ros. ℔β. | pour upon it sweet water 1 pound and rose [water] ½ pound. *— Note: ℔ = libra (pound); ▽ = aqua (water).* |
| 40 | laß 4 tag stehen. hernach d: in MB: dises | Let stand 4 days. Afterwards [distill] in [balneo Mariae]. This *— Note: d: = destillire; MB: = Mariae Balneo (water bath).* |
| 41 | waser leze mit 4 fach tüchlein vber die | water lay with a 4-fold little cloth over the *— Note: leze = netze/lege.* |

## Page 122 — IIIF Canvas #93

**Folio / Side:** recto · **Image:** `b18775275_MS_990_0093.JP2` (5531 × 6853 px) · [IIIF Canvas](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/presentation/b18775275/canvases/b18775275_MS_990_0093.JP2) · [Full-Resolution Image](https://iiif.wellcomecollection.org/image/b18775275_MS_990_0093.JP2/full/full/0/default.jpg) · **Transcription Model:** Gemini 4 Hydrogen

Recto page (two-column layout) continuing the fumigation recipe from p. 121, followed by a section on 'Groser Hiz Vnd Entzündung der Augen' (great heat and inflammation of the eyes) featuring a large watercolour illustration of a nun's head with reddened eyes, four causes of eye inflammation, and recipes for a cooling drink, a laxative drink, an electuary, and an herbal poultice.

### Illustrations and Anatomical Diagrams

**Nun with inflamed eyes** (`#xywh=220,1750,2260,2370`): Half-length/bust watercolour illustration of a nun in a black veil and white wimple with markedly reddened, inflamed eyes.

### Left Column

| Line | German Diplomatic (Original) | English Translation & Editorial Notes |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |

| 2 | sträu ein wais auf ein keüschpan oder gluet | Sprinkle a little on a pinewood splinter or embers *— Note: wais = wenig; keüschpan = Kienspan.* |

| 27 | ℥ij mis. Vnd legbauf. | 2 ounces; mix, and lay upon [the eye]. *— Note: legbauf = legs auf* |
| 28 | **[Rubric] Ein herliches augen Waser in den** | A splendid eye water in |
| 29 | **[Rubric] hizigen Vnd schmerzhafften augen** | hot and painful eyes |
| 30 | ℞. ▽. foenicul ℥j. ▽ Cyano ▽ plantag. ▽ ros | [Take] fennel [water] 1 ounce, cornflower [water], plantain [water], rose [water], |
| 31 | ▽. Valeriana. aā. ℥iß. ♁ alb. ℈ß. | valerian [water], of each 1½ ounces; white [vitriol] ½ scruple, |
| 32 | Crocus. metalor. Gr vj. Tutia pp. ℥ß. | crocus metallorum 6 grains, prepared tutty ½ ounce, |
| 33 | sachari. ♄. ℥ß. XXX. Gr ij. mel. rosac. | sugar of [lead] ½ ounce, [XXX] 2 grains, honey of roses |
| 34 | ℥j. mis. f. Collirium. | 1 ounce; mix, make a collyrium. |
| 35 | Es lindert die Salzige feüchtigkeit. benimbt | It soothes the salty moisture, takes away |
| 36 | die röte Vnd schmerzen. thränen rünglin Vnd | the redness and pain, running of tears, and *— Note: thränen rünglin: slightly uncertain reading* |
| 37 | drücknet es müß aber mit frische dichlin | dries [it]; however, it must be with fresh little cloths |
| 38 | woll auß gedruckht. Vnd warm auf gelegt | well wrung out and laid on warm, |
| 39 | werde. als dan wart allezeit auf rüehret. | and then always stirred up. |
